# Decisions

One dated line per decision. Deviations use `DEVIATION <RULE-ID>` and carry a re-check trigger
(STANDARDS section 0). Newest at the bottom.

- [2026-11-02] DECISION standards: adopted STANDARDS.md v1.0 (copied from pof `games/gtai/STANDARDS.md` at pof 426a731a); from now on this copy is authoritative.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION repo: fresh repo bootstrapped by STANDARDS section 22 steps 1-13 (spark gtai-phase-2 WP3). Local git only; the owner creates the private GitHub repo with LFS and pushes. Spike history is not carried over.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION GIT-01: the repo lives at `C:\Users\kazda\kiro\gtai`, inside the `kiro` working tree (the outer repo sees `gtai/` as untracked), because spark gtai-phase-2 Wave 1 fixed that path. Mitigation: add `gtai/` to `kiro/.git/info/exclude`, and never run a bulk add in `kiro`. Re-check: when the GitHub remote exists, clone it outside `kiro` and retire this line. Owner.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION naming: public strings say "RTAI: Uprising" (Robot Theft A.I.), so `config/name` and the exported `RTAI.exe` use it; code, paths and PoF ids keep `gtai` (spark gtai-phase-2 Wave 2).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION test-framework (TST-02): **GUT 9.7.1** wins over gdUnit4 v6.2.3. The shootout ran on stock 4.7.2 headless with this repo's warnings-as-errors set, one unit test and one scene/input test each, 3 runs each:
  - gdUnit4 v6.2.3 (latest release; v7 with official headless support is unreleased): refuses headless (exit 103) unless `--ignoreHeadlessMode`. Its fluent asserts return a value, so under `return_value_discarded=2` every `assert_x(...).is_y(...)` is a parse error (exit 105) until each test file opts out with `@warning_ignore_start("return_value_discarded")`. After that, 2/2 pass in 2.1-3.0 s, and a planted failure exits 100. Loud on parse errors (good). 516 files, 2.1 MB.
  - GUT 9.7.1 (targets 4.7.x): headless by default. `assert_eq` returns void, so tests compile under the full warning set with no opt-out. 2/2 pass in 1.5-2.8 s (scene input via `GutInputSender`), and a planted failure exits 1. **Hazard:** a test file that fails to parse is skipped with "Nothing was run" and exit 0. `tools/tests/run_unit.mjs` closes it: any SCRIPT ERROR/Parse Error line, or zero tests, fails the run (planted parse error proven to fail). 259 files, 3.0 MB.
  - Why GUT: it is the only one that keeps tests under the same strict warnings as game code, it runs headless without an override, and its 4.7.x pin is published. Re-check at gdUnit4 v7.0 release or any GUT bump.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION gdscript-warnings (GDS-02, 4.7!): 4.7.2 has no `gdscript/warnings/exclude_addons`; it was replaced by `gdscript/warnings/directory_rules = {"res://addons": 0}`. The other ten section 22 setting names were verified present in the 4.7.2 binary.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION strict-warnings idioms: under `return_value_discarded=2`, `PackedStringArray.append` and `Signal.connect` return values, so use typed `Array[String]` (append is void) and check `connect` results. Parse JSON with `JSON.new().parse()`: `JSON.parse_string` raises an engine error on bad input, and GUT counts that as a failure.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION import-gate: `godot --import` exits 0 even when autoloads fail to compile, and `load()` returns a script object even when it failed to compile. `tests/load_all.gd` therefore checks `can_instantiate() or is_abstract()`, and the runners also fail on any SCRIPT ERROR output.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION gitattributes: one pattern per line. The compact `*.a *.b attr` form shown in STANDARDS section 22 makes git ignore every pattern after the first (verified with `git check-attr`). `.gitignore` anchors `/build/` so that `steam/build/` (REL M19 scripts) stays tracked.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION gdtoolkit (GDS-05): pinned 4.5.0. It parses typed dictionaries, variadic args, typed lambdas, `@warning_ignore_start` and `@abstract` (`tools/canary.gd`), so `@abstract` is permitted and used for `PlatformBackend`. gdlint's key is `function-arguments-number` (set to 5). gdlint has no function-length rule, so `tools/style/check_gd.mjs` enforces 40 lines per function.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION pins: Godot publishes SHA-512 sums, not SHA-256, so `versions.toml` pins the official SHA-512 (the local win64 zip and export templates match).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION renderer (STM-11, provisional): `forward_plus` with `d3d12` on Windows, as in the spike. Re-check: the first Proton/Deck measurement of d3d12 vs vulkan.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION SAV-06: the custom user dir is `RTAI Uprising`, with no `<Studio>/` prefix. The owner confirmed it on 2026-11-02, so it is no longer a deviation; the Auto-Cloud path freezes on it at Steam app creation.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION achievements path: the source is `steam/achievements/achievements.json` (ACH-01), not the brief's `data/achievements.json`; STANDARDS takes precedence. The schema is `steam/achievements/achievements.schema.json`.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION ACH-04: the dead-trigger check (no `Events.<on>.emit` site) and the source check (resolving canon/entities) only warn. No gameplay is ported and canon is not reachable from this repo yet. Switch: `DEAD_TRIGGER_IS_ERROR` / `UNRESOLVED_SOURCE_IS_ERROR` in `tools/achievements/rules.mjs`. Re-check: the first Foundation feature port that emits an achievement event. Owner of that ticket.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION platform cloud surface: Steam Auto-Cloud (SAV-07) needs no API, so `PlatformBackend` exposes only `profile_dir()` and `cloud_enabled()` beyond the STM-02 list. NullBackend's mirror is the store and replays `pending` unlocks later (STM-06).
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION NAT-04: the godot-cpp submodule is not added yet; `native/GODOT_CPP_VERSION` and `versions.toml` hold placeholders, and so do GodotSteam (STM-01) and Sentry. Re-check: the native port ticket, the first Steam milestone, and the first external playtest, in that order.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION TST-04: the native-matrix and Windows-export/PCK-audit CI jobs are stubs (`tools/pck_audit` is a README), and the section 22 step 14 export proof run has not been done. Re-check: the first export ticket (F-ticket), which must land `tools/pck_audit` before any external build.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION evidence: `node tools/evidence/run.mjs` writes `build/evidence/{lint,unit-tests}.json` (`pof.godot-evidence/1`) against HEAD. It refuses a dirty tree (unless `--allow-dirty`, which every summary then states), and import churn during the run fails the unit-tests evidence.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION tooling: Node tool scripts are zero-dependency (`tools/lib/mini_schema.mjs` interprets the JSON Schemas). `justfile` mirrors the Node commands, but `just` is not installed on the dev box yet; the Node commands are the source of truth.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION line-endings: gdformat 4.5.0 on Windows writes CRLF into every file it reformats. `.gitattributes` normalises the index to LF, but after formatting run `sed -i 's/\r$//'` on the touched files, or format before the first add, so working copies stay LF too.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION gdtoolkit excludes: `excluded_directories` in gdlintrc/gdformatrc matches bare directory names (`addons`), never paths (`project/addons` silently matches nothing). With `addons` listed, the STANDARDS forms `gdlint project/` and `gdformat --check project/` are clean.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION events registry (F-04): the registry stays at `project/data/schemas/events.json` (ARC-06, section 22 step 11), not the brief's `data/events.json`; STANDARDS takes precedence. Each signal carries `name`, `doc`, `emitter` (a `features/<x>` path), `wired` and typed `params`; lint `events-registry` now compares names and types against `core/events/events.gd`, and `test_events_contract.gd` holds one test per signal.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION events (F-04): added `actor_killed(killer_id, victim_id, kind)`, `wanted_changed(level)`, `cash_changed(amount, delta)`, `contract_started(id)`, `contract_completed(id)`, `vehicle_entered(vehicle_id)`, `vehicle_exited(vehicle_id)`, none wired yet. `soul_swapped(soul_index)` became `soul_swapped(from, to)`, so `ACH_SOUL_1_FIRST_SHIFT` now matches `where to == 1`. Actor and vehicle ids are runtime ints; contract ids and kinds are StringName. `actor_killed` carries a written contract: the payload, kind included, is decided before the damage chokepoint applies the lethal hit, and the chokepoint is the only emitter (the spike set `death_kind` after `_damage()` at evolve `moves.gd:119`).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION GDS-11 (SHOULD): `actor_killed.kind` is a StringName from a closed list in events.json (`blown`, `burned`, `shot`, `stamped`), not an int enum, because achievement `where` clauses match it as data; the list is the boundary, and a new kind is a registry edit.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION emitter wiring (ACH-04, F-04): `gen.mjs validate` reads `wired` and `emitter` from events.json. A wired signal must have a production emit site (`Events.x.emit(`, `ctx.events.x.emit(` or `_events.x.emit(`, comments stripped, `tests/` and `dev/` excluded), all under `project/<emitter>/`; a planned signal must have none; an emit of an unlisted name fails. Planted cases for all four failures were proven to fail. The DEVIATION ACH-04 above narrows: a dead trigger can now only exist on a `wired: false` signal, and only that case warns.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION composition root (ARC-04, F-04): `WorldContext` (`core/world_context.gd`) and the abstract `WorldSystem` (`core/world_system.gd`) live in `core/` because features may depend only on `core/` (LAY-05); `boot/world.gd` (`World`) builds the context once and calls `setup(ctx)` on each system. To type the context, the `Events` and `Data` autoload scripts gained `class_name EventBus` and `class_name DataStore`. The budget is full: 7 of 7 autoloads, enforced by lint `autoload-budget` and a unit test.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION events debug log (ARC-06): `EventLog` records `<tick> <signal> <args json>` for every domain signal; the `Events` autoload keeps a 256-line ring in debug builds only (ticked by `Engine.get_physics_frames` until `World.start` points it at the sim clock). The same class is the replay event timeline.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION tick (SIM-01, F-05): an explicit accumulator in `SimClock`, driven from `World._process`, rather than `_physics_process`, so the clamp, the 4-step cap and the tick counter live in one testable `RefCounted`. The raw frame delta is clamped to 4 ticks (66.7 ms) at `SimClock.advance()`, the one choke point; lint bans any other used `_process`/`_physics_process` delta and `get_*process_delta_time()` outside `core/clock/`. The audit found no other consumer in the repo; the spike's evolve `sfx.gd:177` (music fades and loop TTLs on the raw `_process` dt) is the bug class it closes.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION rng (SIM-02, F-05): `RngRegistry` derives each stream's seed as `(master & 0xFFFFFFFF) << 32 | fnv1a32(name)`, not `String.hash()`, which Godot does not promise stable across versions; a golden-value test pins it. The master seed is 32-bit so it survives JSON (DAT-05). The lint grep now also bans `rand_from_seed`, `.shuffle(`/`.pick_random(` (global RNG), `.randomize(` and `RandomNumberGenerator.new(` outside `core/rng/`; planted lines proved each rule fires.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION replay (SIM-05, F-05): `gtai.replay/1` stores input as changes (tick + packed int), a SHA-256 state hash every 60 ticks, the `Events` timeline and the final hash. `--replay` runs the log synchronously, so `--fixed-fps 60` is unnecessary. The build id is `application/config/version` + engine hash until the export pipeline stamps a git hash. In the game no systems are registered yet, so `--replay` currently verifies an empty world (proven: identical exits 0, a tampered hash exits 1, a missing file exits 2); feature systems must be added in `World._ready` before the replay branch.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION scenario runner (TST-03): GDScript has no exceptions, and a runtime error inside `Scenario.run()` returned a default and passed (seen while building F-05). `run_scenarios.gd` now installs an `ErrorCounter` (`Logger`, 4.5+) and fails any scenario that logs an engine or script error, and refuses a script that does not compile; a planted crashing scenario was proven to fail.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION GDS-03: `tests/support/error_counter.gd` overrides the engine virtual `Logger._log_error`, which has 8 fixed parameters (cap 5); gdlint's `function-arguments-number` is disabled for that one function. Re-check: never, unless Godot changes the virtual's signature. Lane A.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION evidence scope: `tools/evidence/run.mjs` covers lint + GUT (which includes the 5 s replay in `tests/integration/`); the 60 s scenario runs in CI's tests job and by hand, and has no evidence kind yet. Adding a `scenarios` evidence kind is a PoF wire-contract change, left to the Director.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION look (F-08): the D4 slice print pass is `assets/shaders/look_d4.gdshader` (world) + `look_d4_item.gdshader` (items, actors' fx, HUD, ghosts), ported from the spike's artdir wash pass and tuned in the crop tests (`grime` 0.30, `wash_mid` 0.90, `step_hi` 0.84, `line_ink` 0.86). The pen work lives in the D4 art, not the shader.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION texel density, input to F-13 (AST-06): D4 world art is authored at 80 px/m for roof and prop sprites (the spike pack footprints, so derived collision is unchanged) and 96 px/m for 8 m ground tiles (768 px). One district, RGBA8 with mips: ground 39.0 MB, roofs 27.3 MB, props 1.5 MB (at 128 px/m: 69.3, 70.0, 3.8 MB; at 64: 17.3, 17.5, 1.0). The Codex masters hold about 84 px/m of real detail for roofs and 59 px/m for asphalt, so a 128 px/m arm would be an upscale. F-13 decides; the owner signs off.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION GIT-07 / AST-06: the D4 art build scripts (key, template cut, Lanczos fit, re-ink, tile layout, slicer) live with the masters (`gtai-masters/d4/tools`, absolute paths, Python + Pillow + SciPy), not as a pinned step under `tools/`, so CI cannot regenerate the game-ready PNGs yet. Re-check: before the next art wave lands, move them to `tools/art/` with relative paths and a drift check. Owner of the art lane.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION NAT-04: godot-cpp is pinned to commit `e103d348` (master of 2026-10-30, the commit the spike proved with `api_version=4.7`) instead of a tag, because godot-cpp publishes no 4.7 tag (newest: `godot-4.5-stable`). This replaces the earlier NAT-04 placeholder deviation (the submodule now exists). Re-check: the first godot-cpp 4.7 tag. Lane A.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION NAT-05: CI builds MSVC debug + release and runs the standalone core tests and the parity gate on Windows only; the Linux debug build under ASan/UBSan is not in CI, the core tests are not run under sanitizers, and the `.gdextension` Linux entries are untested. Re-check: F-07, or any Linux/Deck-native decision, whichever comes first. Lane A.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION TST-04: the `native` CI job is real (MSVC debug/release, `scons tests`, pinned Godot, `--import`, `run_parity.gd`), so the TST-04 deviation above narrows to the Windows export + PCK-audit job only.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION NAT-08: the shared constants' one source is `tools/gen_constants/sim_constants.json`; it generates both `SimConst` (.gd) and `sim_constants.h`, DO NOT EDIT with the source hash, and lint fails on drift. A hand-written header that GDScript parsed was rejected: two generated files keep both sides typed. Floats must be exact in binary (NAT-07).
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION DAT-01: the sim's tuning literals (speeds, radii, timers, about 60 numbers) still appear in both the GDScript reference and the C++ core, operation for operation; only structural constants are shared. The parity gate catches any drift between them. Re-check: the first ticket that tunes the crowd or traffic (move them into `sim_constants.json` then). Lane A.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION city API (NAT-02): the backend interface is `CitySimBackend` (both backends), and `spawn` takes one `SP_LEN` PackedFloat64Array instead of the spike's 10 parameters (GDS-03 cap 5). Dropped from the spike: the RenderingServer canvas-item draw path (MultiMesh won the A/B), rig slots and `nearest_people` (rigs are F-09). The native class is looked up by name, so every script compiles where the library is not built. The binding validates its arguments.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION city art: no spike art is ported. `data/tables/city_textures.json` keeps the 65 draw-list ids with their metre sizes (measured from the spike art) and solidity; `art` is null and `CityTextures` draws flat placeholders until the art lane lands shipped textures with provenance (LEG-05, GIT-03; the spike ped atlas is 2048 x 5504, over AST-05). `features/crowd/` stays empty: the crowd sim lives with the bubble in `features/city/` (LAY-05).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION chunks (AST-11/12): GTCH files are git-ignored build output (`project/data/generated/city/`, 8 MB); the committed golden list is `data/generated/city_chunks.sha256`. A missing file is generated in memory (5.7 ms/chunk, byte-identical); a header naming an older generator version is reported and regenerated; a bad magic/version/key/hash is never instantiated. The generator uses exact quarter turns, not libm `sin`/`cos`, so the golden list holds on every platform.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION streaming determinism (SIM-05, WLD-02): which chunks the SIM holds (`CityResidency`) is a pure function of the focus, evaluated per tick; worker prefetch only makes it cheaper. The spike stamped chunks when a time-sliced activation finished, so its sim depended on frame timing. Drawing (`ChunkView`) keeps the 1.5 ms slice and pooled items.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION EXP-04: a fourth export preset, `windows-bench` (release template, `bench` feature, a main-scene override to `dev/bench/city_bench.tscn`, ships `dev/bench` and the baked chunks), exists only to measure WLD-08 on an exported release build; it is never shipped. Re-check: the first export ticket (fold it into the `devtools` build if that proves equivalent). Lane A.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION bench (WLD-08): measured on the exported release build, windowed (D3D12, RTX 4090, Ryzen 7800X3D, vsync off, uncapped, a shared machine), 60 s each, the first 120 frames excluded. The crowded drive runs at 9.6 px/m (zoom 0.3), wider than gameplay zoom, so that about 50 actors (max 141) are on screen while streaming at up to 25 m/s; the riot is 300 rioters + 100 cops at 32 px/m. `--headless` exists for CPU-only runs and says so. Development run (`build/evidence/bench.json` at HEAD is authoritative): drive p99 2.30 ms, 1% low 317 fps, worst swap 3.98 ms, worst frame 32.6 ms; riot p99 2.48 ms, 1% low 336 fps, worst swap 2.09 ms, worst frame 41.4 ms (outside every swap window: the open stall class, F-07).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION evidence: `run.mjs` also writes `scenario-tests`, `parity` and `bench` (`bench` metrics `p99CpuMs`, `low1Fps`, `worstSwapMs`, `worstFrameMs` = the worse scenario, plus `drive.*` / `riot.*` detail and `exported`). `--no-parity` / `--no-bench` skip a kind without rewriting its file, so PoF sees it stale, never a fake pass; the Linux CI evidence job uses both. Bench status fails on a section 20 hard limit or a chunk failure.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION GDS-08: `dev/` SceneTree runners (bake, bench) may `printerr`, like the test runners: autoloads are not reachable when `-s` scripts compile.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION look retune (f08b, owner: streets missed the target frame): charcoal streets against cream pavements and roofs. The asphalt tiles are re-toned to a 0.30 base and `look_d4` prints them in its dark wash step (`step_hi` 0.68, `line_ink` 0.90, `grime` 0.15); the roofs get a Codex cross-hatching edit of the approved sheets; `features/characters/print_part.gdshader` ships with a D4 flat-tint mode (limb cream prints as a light wash) for the rig port.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest loader (F-12, DAT-05/07): `features/quest/` loads `gtai.quest/1` with the `JSON` class only (SEC-02) and validates every field the runtime reads at the boundary, with its JSON path, against PoF `types.ts` (closed `act`/`status`/`purpose` lists, `speaker` must be a listed character, `story.writes` shape) and against what this runtime can play; a typed `QuestPackage` is built only when there are no errors. The verbs, fail-state keyword rules, bark condition vocabulary and timing moved from spike literals into `data/tables/quest_rules.json` (`gtai.quest-rules/1`, DAT-01). Rejected: bulk-copying the spike's Dictionary-based runtime (untyped, GDS-01).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest fail rules: fail-state phrases match keyword rules on whole words, not substrings. The spike's substring match mapped the PoF fixture's `cartridge destroyed` to `car_destroyed`, so wrecking the car would have failed a quest with no car.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION LOC-02 / DAT-08: a quest line's words arrive in the package (`lines[].text`), not in PO. `QuestText` gives each quest string a symbolic key (`QUEST__<quest>__LINE__<line>`, `QUEST__<quest>__BEAT__<beat>`, `CAST__<character>`, LOC-01), resolves it through `tr()` with the `@joke` overlay (LOC-06), and falls back to the package English instead of `!!KEY!!` while no PO carries the key. Re-check: the PO pipeline ticket (`tools/po_lint` is a stub), which extracts quest text into `en.po` and drops the fallback.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest runtime (F-12, LAY-05/ARC-08): `QuestSystem` is a `WorldSystem` that plays one contract through an injected `QuestWorld` port (position, vehicle, wanted, cash, soul, madness, actors), not through `main.gd` fields as in the spike; wanted is capped and cash paid by asking the world, whose implementation must use the owning feature's one setter. Time is ticks (`SimClock`), seconds are converted once at load (rounded up). The runtime draws no random numbers, so it takes no RNG stream (a stream with no draws would only add state-hash noise); the first random choice takes `ctx.rng.stream(&"quest")`. Re-check: integration with the city, player and police features, which supplies the real `QuestWorld`.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION events (F-12): `contract_started` and `contract_completed` are `wired`, and their emitter moved from `features/missions` to `features/quest`, where the quest runtime lives (the brief's path). The contract id is the package id (kebab, for example `collect-call`), so an achievement `where id ==` clause must use that form, not the `m01_clock_out` style in `test_events_contract.gd`.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest lines (F-12): line and bark timing (typewriter at 45 chars/s, a 1.4 s hold, clamped to 2.2-9 s) is computed from the package's source text in ticks, not from the translated string, so the sim and its replay hash do not depend on the player's locale. Barks that become eligible on the same tick queue in package order (the spike's `push_front` loop reversed them). The HUD drawing (memo card, passed/scrapped stamp, payphone and objective markers) is not ported; `QuestLinePlayer.subtitle()` and `QuestObjective.marker_position()` are what the HUD feature reads. Re-check: the HUD port.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION scenario evidence (F-12): `run_scenarios.gd` takes `--evidence-dir=<dir>` (`Scenario.write_evidence()`), and `tools/tests/run_scenarios.mjs` passes `build/evidence/`, so the quest selftest's `quest-selftest.json` (`gtai.quest-selftest/1`) is an artifact of the `scenario-tests` envelope, whose `metrics` gain `questPass`, `questTicks`, `questBeatsReached`, `questLinesShown`, `questChecksPassed` and `questChecksTotal`. The `pof.godot-evidence/1` envelope is unchanged (no new kind); a stale detail file is deleted before each run, so a scenario that does not write never shows an old pass.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest scenario (F-12 exit): `scenario_quest_selftest` plays the spike's `_selftest` package on the real `World` + `SimLoop` against `tests/support/stub_quest_world.gd` (no city, as briefed), with `QuestBot` moving Rivet each tick where the spike teleported him. A second play must match in state hash and Events timeline, and the planted fault (the phone is never answered) must be caught (TST-05).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION stall attribution (F-07, TEL-03): WPR needs an elevated session (kernel providers), which this lane does not have, so frames are attributed in-engine. `HitchRecorder` (owned by every World in the tree) records each frame over 25 ms to `user://logs/hitches.jsonl` with wall, game, render CPU/GPU, draw-phase time (`frame_pre_draw` to `frame_post_draw`, which ends in the swap), pipeline compiles (the five `PIPELINE_COMPILATIONS_*` monitors) and the gap of `FrameCanary`, a 1 ms sleeper thread. Causes: `outside` (the canary stalled too: the whole process was descheduled), `pipeline`, `game`, `render`, `present` (the draw phase waited far beyond the render server's work), else `unexplained`.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION stall finding (F-07): on the exported release build every frame over 25 ms after warm-up was `present`: trios of ~32 ms frames two frames apart (two 60 Hz vblanks), with game work under 0.2 ms and render CPU under 0.3 ms, the canary on time, and no hitch at all headless. An A/B at 48 px/m (30 s each) gave 12 hitches with vsync off and 0 with vsync on or mailbox. Cause: DXGI blocking the uncapped, tearing swap chain under the compositor; it is the spike's "trios of ~32 ms frames" and its source of the 30-48 ms class. The shipped presentation is vsync on (the project default), and the bench adds a vsync-on drive to prove it. The 165-440 ms whole-process stalls of the spike's first batch did not reproduce; the canary would label them `outside`. The one in-game cause found is the first-frame pipeline compile (25-89 ms), now moved to the loading screen by `CityView.prewarm()` (the World holds the sim until it finishes).
- [2026-11-02] DECISION `present` frames over 50 ms (F-07): a frame attributed `present` (the draw phase, which ends in the swap, took the frame while the render server's CPU work and our game work were small and the canary ran on time) is explained: the process was running and blocked in the driver/compositor's swap chain. The bench gate counts only `unexplained` frames over 50 ms; `present`, `outside` and every other cause are reported per cause in `bench.json`. Re-check: a `present` frame over 50 ms in a vsync-on run on the Deck (Proton/Vulkan), which would make it a shipping problem.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION bench scenarios (F-07, WLD-08): four per pass, three consecutive passes per evidence run: `drive` (the 9.6 px/m stress case, ~50 actors on screen), `drive48` (the in-car 48 px/m camera at speed with streaming; the same 400 actors in a tighter bubble, ra 45 / rb 80 / spawn ring from 36 m, so the 40 x 25 m view holds ~15, max ~28; the default bubble showed ~3), `riot` (400 on foot), and `drive48vsync` (the same at the as-shipped vsync on). The CPU limits (p99, 1% low, swap) are judged on the three uncapped runs; the vsync run's frame time is the display's and feeds only the hitch counts and the worst frame.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION flow field (WLD-09, F-07): one integer BFS over the 1 m cells, 160 x 160 m centred on the police target, neighbours E S W N, 16-bit distances, rebuilt every 15th tick only while police are alive and only when the target cell or the map version changes; cops beyond 10 m walk the downhill neighbour (fixed tie-break order, diagonals only between two open sides, straight-line fallback) and police cars take the junction exit with the smallest field distance. Identical in both backends (parity exact). Native build 100-150 us, worst 329 us (budget 2 ms).
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION nav budget (section 20): the GDScript reference builds the flow field in 21-31 ms, so on the fallback path (no native library) one tick in 15 is a hitch. The shipped path is native (worst 0.33 ms). Re-check: if the GDScript fallback is ever shipped. Lane A.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION art wiring: 43 of 65 texture ids draw D4 art (ground, roofs, props); `size_m` is unchanged, so the generator and the golden chunk hashes are untouched. Still placeholders: the rounded kerb corner (id 10, lane B has no `curb_corner_tone` yet), vehicles 44-53 and decals 54-64; people and moving cars are placeholder discs and bodies until the rig port (F-09). The look is `look_d4` (world pass, `world_px` every frame) under `look_d4_item` (actors), on by default.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION selftest evidence: `dev/selftest` renders a junction at 32 and 48 px/m with the debug editor binary in a window (release templates refuse a scene argument, and the bench preset's main scene is the bench) and checks size, colour count and actors on screen; `selftest.json` carries both PNGs as artifacts. Re-check: the export ticket (an exported selftest).
- [2026-11-02] CORRECTION (F-07): the earlier note that vsync on removes the present-wait hitches rested on one 30 s test. The 3-run x 4-scenario bench disproves it: the vsync-on drive still had 13 present-attributed frames over 25 ms (worst 39.6 ms, none over 50 ms). Present-wait hitches stay an open TEL item, re-checked at the slice-exit bench and on a real Deck. Director.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION characters (F-09b): the cutout rig is render-side (`features/characters/`): `CharacterRigs` gives full rigs to the 16 nearest people within 14 m (Rivet always), steps them on the 60 Hz tick from `ctx.clock` (the nearest 6 every tick, the rest on alternate ticks with a double step, the spike's crowd LOD), draws no RNG stream and writes no replay state. Pools per body plan are prewarmed at load (1 Rivet + 16 cops + 16 peds; 33 rigs in 4-90 ms, the high figure when the part textures load cold); a look change is a texture swap. Everyone else is drawn from `CrowdAtlas` (8 looks x idle/walk/flee/hit/death/shoot baked from the real rig, 3200 x 1024); `CrowdAtlas.clip_table()` is the city's `CitySimRender.setup()` clip table and `crowd_atlas.gdshader` reads instance custom.x as the cell. Lane A integrates by filling a `RigFeed` per tick.
- [2026-11-02] DEVIATION section 20 rig cost: measured (headless, editor build, a shared box at about 38% load from other processes) p50 0.42-0.55 ms and p99 0.78-0.99 ms per tick for 16 full rigs at 50 and 200 people, inside the 1 ms hard limit but above the 0.3 ms target. The scenario gates the median only (p99 is flaky on a shared box); `dev/bench/rig_bench.gd` records p99. Re-check: an exported-build run on an idle machine, then a native (GDExtension) pose step if the target still misses. Owner of the characters feature.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest vocabulary (F-12b): `quest_rules.json` is now `gtai.quest-rules/2` and plays collect-call's verbs, each with its own handler: `evade` (done at wanted 0; at a place whose marker has a payphone it then rings and must be answered), `answer`, `meet` (waits for the beat's lines: the meeting is the conversation), `fight` and `sabotage` (the world's encounter at the place, then an interact for sabotage). Their `target` is prose and is not resolved; `location` names a place the world's table resolves by the package's own words. Payphones and encounters are facts about places that the world supplies (`phone`, `encounters`), never parsed from prose. `arrived_hot` maps the package's "answers or arrives while heat is above 0" as a gate on answer/meet/payphone evade, not a failure. Rejected: narrowing the council output (owner-approved package) and parsing objective prose.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION events (F-12b, ARC-06): five planned signals (`wired: false`) back the event-name bark tags: `wave_started`, `boss_engaged`, `attack_telegraphed`, `player_hit` (Rivet only, ARC-07) from `features/combat`, and `whispers_spiked` from `features/characters`. `kill` and `heat-dropping` use the F-04 `actor_killed` (killer = Rivet) and `wanted_changed` (falling); `crates-burning` is the contract's own `sabotage` completing. A tag holds for `event_window_s` (2 s) after its fact, so `robo-tazer-hit` + `static-whisper-burst` can both hold. 16 of 40 signals.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest barks (F-12b): a bark listed in a beat's `lineIds` is armed from that beat on; a bark in no beat is armed for the whole contract (the `_selftest` barks). Arming every bark at start, as the spike did, would fire b3's `rivet-buildings-talk` on b1's first whisper.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest offer (F-12b): a contract rings only when its first place's marker has a payphone; otherwise it begins on offer (collect-call picks up straight from the factory). The spike fell back to a fixed yard payphone.

- [2026-11-02] DECISION quest packages (F-12b, DAT-09): `collect-call` is copied verbatim from PoF into `project/quests/collect-call/quest.json` (per the PoF quest README), and every export preset's `include_filter` gains `quests/*.json`. Place names stay the world's contract (the city lane's places table), which PoF cannot check.
- [2026-11-02] DECISION rig in the city (F-14, LAY-05): the glue between `features/city` and `features/characters` is `boot/city_characters.gd` (`CityCharacters`, the composition root), so neither feature depends on the other. It fills the `RigFeed` from the sim's people within 18 m of the focus plus Rivet each frame, steps `CharacterRigs` on the fixed tick (its own `_process` is off, so selection and the crowd write happen in the same frame), and hands the sim the rigged slots. The sim gained three render-only calls in both backends (`set_rig_slots`, `set_person_px`, `get_variants`); none is sim state, so parity and replays are unchanged. The backend interface now has 24 public methods and opts out of gdlint's `max-public-methods` (20) in its three files.

Added as small tested additions, not a rewrite: five handlers in `QuestJobObjective` (new file, dispatched from `QuestObjective`), `QuestCondition` kinds `CUE` and `WEAPON` with their vocabulary in `quest_rules.json` (`cues`, `weapons`), and four non-abstract `QuestWorld` methods (`cue_raised`, `weapon_name`, `ask`, `reached`) so the stub and older worlds compile unchanged. `test_quest_rules.gd` asserted the old "no ':'" / "unknown condition 'weapon'" errors; those cases now read "unknown cue" / "unknown weapon". - [2026-11-03] DECISION vehicle handling feel (d-vehicles): grip-limited steering, |v * yaw| <= 0.85 * grip without the handbrake, so full lock at speed carves an arc instead of spinning (first trace: a 23 m/s full-lock turn needed 46 m/s^2 against 18 of grip and spun into a wall); the handbrake is the way to rotate hard (1.35x yaw, grip x handbrake_grip). Wall hits hurt once per tick by the worst normal speed (summing the 6 hull points wrecked a car in one kerb hit). per_dv 3.5 (a 22 m/s head-on = D2, not a wreck). Rejected: a slip-angle tyre curve (more knobs than the slice needs).

Car-on-car damage uses its own `ram_per_dv` (5.5) beside the wall `per_dv` (3.5): rams read as carnage in two hits, a kerb scrape never wrecks. Impacts spin a car by the lever arm of the touching circle (capped 4 rad/s, bled 5 rad/s^2). The wheel is in the replay state. Why: slice-play's LiveInput maps keys to full lock in one tick; without a slew a tap snaps the nose (golden path steering-feel-profile-shaping: turn-in slower than release, heavier cars slower). Native `car_pass.cpp` + GDScript twin `CitySimCarPass`; the steering target is now a per-step lane line (`Car::ln`). Parity re-places its obstacle list every 300 steps (was 40) so cars stand long enough to pull out: EXACT over 1200 steps, 6 pass starts in the run, mutation still caught at 629; no replay or look golden moved (no golden scene has service cars). scenario_vehicle_yield: the three cars behind a parked sedan queued 80.5 s and never passed before, now 13.6 s and 3 of 3 pass (first at 9.25 s), 0 rear-ends; the scenario now fails if fewer than 2 pass. Why: a parked or wrecked player car blocked a lane until the stuck timer despawned the queue (20 s, sometimes on screen). Rejected: a lateral creep past within the own lane (roads are 8 m, two 4 m lanes: no room) and despawning queued cars sooner (visible pops). Known limits: an oncoming car that appears during the pass makes both stop until the 6 s abort; queues only drain one car at a time (only the car directly behind an obstacle pulls out). Re-check when roads get more than two lanes or when traffic lights land.

- [2026-11-03] DECISION gunplay: the hit flash in the D5 print is an inked impact star (a cream 8-point star with an ink rim, about 2.3 m across at the hit, shrinking over the class's flash_ticks) drawn behind the victim, not a white tint. Multiplicative modulate cannot brighten an ink sprite without HDR 2D, and a comic impact star is the D5 language. Knockback is a decaying push on the victim (`HitLink`, x0.82 per tick, so a 1.5 m/s pistol push slides a body about 0.13 m), so a kill reads as a shove. Rejected: a tint toward red (red is blood only) or toward cream (invisible on cream rims).
- [2026-11-03] DECISION gunplay (readable enemy fire on the real fight): with a table gun wired, quest 1's contractors fire through `GuardTell` (stop, 0.35 s tell: a dashed vermilion aim line that tracks, then a solid line with a cream muzzle glint for the lock, then the burst down the locked line at enemy_fire.speed_scale). The locked burst keeps `enemy_fire.lock_spread_scale` = 0.3 of the rifle cone, so the drawn line is the danger zone (realtime-combat-semantics: a telegraph must not under-draw the threat). Measured on the armored dock fight: standing 60/60 rounds land, strafing on the lock 17/48; the full cone (1.0) gives 25/60 and 9/48. Rejected for now: the full cone (luck over the read). Owner ask gunplay-3 (default A = 0.3). Quest 1 keeps GuardBrain (guard_tell null), goldens unchanged.
- [2026-11-03] DECISION gunplay: `GuardTell.one_slot` (one contractor tells or bursts at a time) exists as an A/B switch, default off. Measured on the armored dock fight with the tight burst: strafing on the lock lands 9/36 with the slot vs 17/48 without (crossfire), at a quarter fewer rounds. The default waits for an owner look at the side-by-side clip; attack-slot ownership is enemy-ai's (encounter pacing), GuardTell only honours it.
- [2026-11-03] DECISION gunplay: `GuardTell.one_slot` stays off. The crossfire seen at 17/48 was the scripted dodger's alternating side walking into a second gunner's line, not too many gunners at once: with the side picked away from the other live gunners (pistol_fight `--dodge-away`), the armored dock fight lands 8/42 enemy rounds (pistol) and 8/33 (machine pistol) with every contractor free to burst, as good as one_slot's 9/36 without slowing the pace by a quarter. Rejected: one_slot on by default (slower fight for the same safety). Re-check: when enemy-ai's encounter director owns attack slots, or a real player's dodge (TickInput) lands more than 1 in 4.
- [2026-11-03] DECISION gunplay (evidence): pistol_fight `--ppm=<n>` snaps the CameraRig to the camera.json mode with that ppm; the earlier view-only setting never reached the screen, so iteration 4's '48 px/m' clip was the 32 px/m one (byte-identical stills) and is withdrawn. Re-check: when the rig gets a free ppm override, use it instead of borrowing the car mode.
- [2026-11-03] DECISION gunplay (owner answer gunplay-3, 22:55): A, tight burst on the told line. `weapons.json` enemy_fire.lock_spread_scale stays 0.3 (already the default; no data change, goldens unchanged). This is an owner pick on real clips, not a placeholder. Director notes from the stills, carried to the night run: at 32 px/m the pistol in Rivet's claw barely reads; live contractors clump with corpses into one blob, so the living must stay readable (corpse tint/fade or an outline on the living).
- [2026-11-03] DECISION enemy-ai: enemies are sim data in `features/combat/ai/` (EnemyPit, one 60 Hz tick, `enemy_ai` RNG stream for shot spread only), archetypes in `data/tables/enemies.json` (patrol cop, riot cop, SWAT, robot thug, robot gunner). Coordination is attack tokens with a lease (max_shooters 2, max_melee 2, token_hold_s 2.5), not a commander (registry agent-behaviour-authoring: coordination as a claim on a scarce resource). Every attack has a tell; a gun's aim tracks until aim_lock_s 0.2 before the shot, then locks, so moving dodges (measured: hit rate 0.50 standing, 0.24 reading tells, 0.34 with the lock off). Shot accuracy is a per-archetype chance-to-hit curve by range, turned into an aim cone (atan(r/d)/p). Rejected: reusing GuardBrain/CombatArena (quest 1's one-hit spray AI, kept as is for the quest); per-group claim tables (only one defender today; the registry's rule of putting the claims on the defender is noted for when the arena has more than Rivet as a target). Open: enemy toughness (ask enemy-ai-1, default A as built).
- [2026-11-03] DECISION enemy-ai waves: pit waves are data (`data/tables/enemy_waves.json`, gtai.enemy_waves/1): three named curves (gentle, standard, steep), each a list of waves of {arch, count, gate}; `curve` picks the live one; a wave starts breather_s 3 after the last one is cleared, enemies walk in spawn_gap_s 0.6 apart with the gates interleaved (several sides at once). The pit default is `steep` (ASK enemy-ai-2, default B, pending): against the scripted human-like Rivet (react 0.25 s, 70 % of tells noticed, 4 deg aim error) only steep draws damage (0 / 4 / 20 per wave). Rejected: reusing features/combat/wave_director.gd (quest 1's ring-spawn director, tied to CombatArena). Also: EnemySenses.detour skips a corner already reached and tests reachability against the body-grown box (two stalls found by the wave runs: a walker re-picking the corner it stands on, and a body pinned on a box face by push-out).

The patrol cop keeps hp 1 (humans die to one nail, owner doctrine): its hit reaction is the DEATH clip shoved along the blow plus blood. Re-check: when a robot-thug plan and art exist, or the arena scene lands on this branch.
- [2026-11-03] DECISION enemy-ai suppression: a Rivet nail that passes a human enemy within 0.6 m (and has flown past it, so a hit next tick is never pre-empted) makes it flinch away from the nail: a sideways rig shove with no hit flash and no blood, so a miss reads unlike a hit. A flinch during the tell (WINDUP/FIRE) breaks it for 14 ticks (the aim line drops, the squad token goes back): shooting near a gunman buys the player a beat, the only non-lethal reaction a one-nail patrol cop can show. Robots never flinch, nor a shield bearer facing the nail; a 45-tick refractory gap stops one burst pinning an enemy (`features/combat/ai/enemy_suppress.gd`). Rejected: a separate SUPPRESSED state with its own timers (the STAGGER path already cancels a windup and returns the token); a RigClips flinch clip (a `features/characters` file). Re-check: when the rig gains a crouch/duck clip, or gunplay's hit-feel module lands.
- [2026-11-03] DECISION enemy-ai aim lead: while a gun's aim tracks (before the lock) it leads a moving Rivet by `squad.aim_lead` 0.8 of his travel over the lock plus the round's flight (enemies.json), so a steady strafe is no longer immunity and the dodge stays a read-and-react act (juke after the lock). Found by the rig clips: cops 0 hits in 14-17 shots on a strafing Rivet. Also the human-like PitDummy no longer dodges every shot at zero reaction (it dodges only shots whose tell it noticed); the perfect-reader judges are unchanged. Rejected: raising accuracy (a wider cone punishes standing still, not strafing). Re-check: the owner's ask on lead vs no lead (night run, real-time clips), and when Rivet's real movement replaces the dummy.

- [2026-11-04] DECISION weapon FX scale (n-weapon-art): FX cells (muzzle flashes, tracer, rocket, flame, arcs, plasma, rings, shard) are authored in rig part px with a 13 px ink ring and draw at `fx_draw_scale` 2.0 x the part scale (`WeaponArt.fx_scale()`), matching the 1.5x drawn weapons. At 1.0 the pistol flash was a speck at 64 px/m and the cream tracer vanished on cream paving (`gtai-masters/n4/weapon-art/frames/lineup-core-fire-*.png`). Still weak at 32 px/m on cream: a darker tracer core is the next pass.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION real-fight area model (night gunplay): `FightAreas` (features/combat/weapons) gives `CombatArena` the weapon table's behaviours through `GunHand` rounds (`Shot.spec`): rocket splash with linear falloff and a knockdown at knockback >= 4 m/s (54 ticks), flame and molotov burning (panic run away from the fire at 1.7x walk, weaving; no shooting while burning, stunned or down), fire patches that re-light whoever stands in them, the electro chain 0.7x per hop and its stun. The bolt chains body to body in the fight (humans conduct; metal props bridge a hop) - `FightAreas.CHAIN_THROUGH_HUMANS = false` restores the range model's metal-only chain. Death kinds by weapon kind: rocket `blown`, flame and molotov `burned`, the rest `shot`. Built lazily with the first GunHand shot, so quest 1 without a GunHand has none and its replay/parity goldens do not move (scenarios 28/28 identical). Rejected: a second copy of Impact over Fighters inside combat_arena.gd (file size, two models drifting).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION burn is lethal for humans (night gunplay): `dot_s` 3 -> 3.5 for flamethrower and molotov, so 30 dps x 3.5 s = 105 >= a human's 100: any touch of fire ends in a panic run and a burned death about 3.5 s later (the readable kill). At 3 s a glancing puff left a human alive at 2 hp. Alternative = one data value back to 3.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION kill payload (night gunplay, JOURNAL REQUEST 1 -> brutality, human-reactions): `CombatArena.kill_landed(victim, weapon, dir, at, kind)` from the damage chokepoint right after `Events.actor_killed`; `damage()` takes the death kind as an optional fifth argument (default `shot`).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION human-reactions: the rig hit flash (print_part `tint`) prints everything but the ink cream, not vermilion: red is blood only and vermilion is danger/objectives/collar (rivet-arsenal REQUEST 1). Rejected: an ink flash (a victim reads as a black hole at 32 px/m).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION human-reactions: gunplay's lasting area states hold their reaction family on the rig while they last (ReactLink.HOLD: stun loops 0.3-0.7 of its family, burn 0.3-0.8, knockdown lies at 0.6 and gets up from 0.78 when down_ticks ends), and kill_landed's kind overrides the weapon class for the death (burned -> death_burn, blown -> death_blast). A hit while knocked down plays no flinch (it would stand the body up for a frame).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: every fire death of a human takes the char kill (signature_kills.json row `burn_char`, keyed on gunplay's payload weapon `burn`, and on kind `burned` for direct flame-puff kills): flamethrower, molotov, fire patch, burning alike. Rejected: flamethrower only (a burning guard killed by a fire patch would die differently from one killed by the puff). Alternative = drop the kind fallback in SignatureKills.on_kill.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: a signature kill vetoes the standard death blood (CombatBlood.veto -> SignatureKills.marked_near; the pool, spurts and CorpseStains soak): the kill prints its own blood, and a fire kill none. CombatBlood's actor_killed bleed is deferred one idle call only when a veto is installed (actor_killed fires before kill_landed). Rejected: keep the pool under gibs (read as a second body in the rocket clips).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: the burnt body is the victim's own rig copy printed through `kill_char.gdshader` (two charcoal tones from the art's lightness + grain). weapon-art's `charred.png` sticker is a front view and does not fit the top-down rig; `ash.png` is used as the ash heap.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: the electro signature kill (`electro_shock`) strobes weapon-art's front-view X-ray skeleton sticker over the top-down body as a comic flash (2 of every 5 ticks, off under --reduced-motion), then drops the body stiff. Rejected for tonight: waiting for a top-down skeleton (REQUEST 4 -> weapon-art).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: the katana signature kill (`katana_split`, style `split`) cuts the victim's own rig in two along a diagonal (two body copies, each clipped to a half-plane by a stretched white Sprite2D; a Polygon2D clip parent drew nothing in 4.7) and keys on weapon id `katana` at CombatArena.damage; until rivet-arsenal wires melee weapons live, the KillLab drives it with a lunge-slash. - [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: on the city block (no print stage) the signature kills draw on the CityView's actor layer, and every CombatBlood under the arena game takes the veto; the city death cue defers its bleed past kill_landed. - [2026-11-04] DECISION brutality: the plasma rifle signature kill (`plasma_hole`) punches a round hole through the body copy with a holed clip mask (the street shows through) and a cooling glow ring; cauterised, no blood. Rejected: a drawn dark disc (reads as a stain, not a hole).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION live melee and motion (night lane rivet-arsenal): StagePilot (quest 1, the arena, the city proving block) drives PlayerMotion (walk, held sprint 6.2 m/s, dodge roll with i-frames gated through `Fighter.exposed`) and the claw combo (PlayerMelee over rivet_melee.json, hit test on the first active tick through `CombatArena.damage`) in place of TopDownMover and the 30-tick electroshock jab. ### 2026-11-04 Rivet baseline speed

- [2026-11-04] DECISION rivet speed (owner: "Default movement is slow, I would increase baseline of main character with 50% increase"): Rivet's walk 4.5 -> 6.75 m/s (combat.json rivet walk_mps) and sprint 6.2 -> 9.3 m/s (rivet_motion.json sprint_mps; sprint accel 16 -> 24 m/s2 so walk-to-sprint keeps its 0.11 s). The tackle line moves with him: bodies.json rivet_base run_mps 5.0 -> 7.5, so the walk still shoves and only the sprint tackles. The gait keeps the evening's pose and stride (RivetPlan GAIT_SCALE 1.3, SPRINT_STRIDE 0.2): the new BodyPlan.speed_k (Rivet 1.5, the Hauler 1.0) scales the gait's walk/run/sprint blend speeds, and the phase still follows the distance, so the feet stay planted and the cadence rises 1.5x (about 3.6 steps/s walking, 4.1 sprinting). Kept: the walk accel 30 / brake 40 m/s2 (time to the new top 0.23 s, was 0.15 s); scaling them 1.5x stalled scenario_rivet_city_fight's bot (wave 2, 4 kills), with either change alone green. Bot glue: ArenaRivetBot's goto steps ease in (v = sqrt(2 brake d)), its sprint step is 50 ticks (75 at 6.2: the same 4.6 m) and its opening stand 20 ticks (40): the claw cops close in on Rivet from tick 0, so the claw (10/10) and chainsaw (3 kills) scenarios hang on when he reaches each station; of nine timelines tried only this one held claw, chainsaw and katana together (fragile, as the claw cops' own note says); scenario/unit speed constants follow the tables. Camera: every live view locks on Rivet's position, so he stays framed (48 px/m clip checked). No stored replay or parity golden moved (replays are recorded and re-run within each scenario; parity is the city sim; the look goldens hold no Rivet motion), so nothing was re-blessed. Rejected: a longer stride for the speed (the owner chose 1.3 as the look; 1.95 would reach past the guard).

### 2026-11-04 corpse decay

- [2026-11-04] DECISION corpse decay (owner: "Dead bodies will need mechanism to decay/disappear so we can bring hoards of opponents without damaging visibility in longer fights"): `features/combat/decay/` with data in `corpse_decay.json`. Two DecayLedgers on the 60 Hz tick, keyed by fighter id, no RNG: bodies (linger 6 s, fade 1.2 s, cap 16, early fade of the oldest past cap - room 4, hard cap releases the most faded and counts it as forced) and stains (linger 12 s, fade 4 s, cap 16). CorpseDecayHook is a station-only arena hook (`arena_stations/corpse-decay.json`, arena, pit and city block): it books deaths, folds both ledgers into the replay hash and takes released bodies out of `CombatArena.hostiles`, except fixtures (anything standing on the first tick: dummies, claw cops, lab guards; they stand up again), which are only hidden until alive. CorpseFade (render) extends the existing systems instead of adding a parallel one: rigs go back to RigPool, ArenaRigs forgets the id, KillView puts each kill's gib groups under a holder and frees them (brutality's leftover budget stays as the ring behind it), the dust pile shrinks with the body, KillGround prints fade with the stain, corpse soaks fade with their body (CorpseStains.body_alpha), and the blood decals and pools take the stain life (BloodFx.set_decal_life; quest 1 keeps 60 s + 120 s). The look: the body inks out (tint to ink by half the fade) and sinks into its pool (shrinks to 5 %). Rejected: an alpha fade. The D5 part print discards a part under alpha 0.45 and stipples it above, so the first try read as a cream checker silhouette and then a pop (frames in gtai-masters/n4/play/frames/). Evidence: scenario_corpse_horde (60 dead, at most 9 drawn and 9 listed against cap 16, 51 pruned, no forced pops, replay identical; planted fault: a linger that never expires keeps 60 listed); a real-time 48 px/m horde of 63 dead held 16.67 ms mean frame time throughout; clip `gtai-masters/n4/play/horde-corpse-decay-48ppm.mp4`. Dev: `--horde` (ArenaPit.horde: waves 4 +2 up to 10, 0.5 s apart, Rivet 50x hp, an on-screen body counter), `--no-decay`. Re-check: the owner's verdict on the ink-and-sink look.

### 2026-11-04 day lane perf-city (city block fight)

- [2026-11-04] DECISION perf measure: the city fight is measured by `FightFrames` (dev arena `--frames=<json> --frames-ticks=2100`, `evidence/perf/fight.sh`, reel ch 18's exact args), per frame: wall, all-scripts process, sim loop, flush, draw (pre -> post draw, which includes the present), event pump, physics, render CPU/GPU, pipelines, tick. "After the first second" means after 1000 ms of wall time. Real-time films use ddagrab on the window's client rect (`evidence/perf/film_rt.sh`); gdigrab's title mode films a D3D12 window black. Rejected: `--fixed-fps` films for lag (every frame is one tick there, so lag cannot show).
- [2026-11-04] FINDING for showcase (reel ch 18 "lagging"): reel-1012's city chapter is a fixed-fps film, its camera pans smoothly and its timestamps are clean. The "lag" is two still stretches (2.4 s and 0.9 s) between pit waves, where no guard is standing and `CombatReel._pit` parks Rivet at its spot. That is pacing, not frame cost (clip: gtai-masters/d6/perf-city/ch18-still-stretch-reel1012.mp4). Re-check when the reel is re-cut.
- [2026-11-04] FINDING perf (city fight, exported release, a1275c82): UNCONTENDED vsync off p99 5.9-6.6 ms, 1 % low 111-125 fps, sim loop 1-2 ms, all scripts 2.5-4 ms. The p99 < 8.3 ms target holds without a code change. The frames over 33 ms after the first second (0-2 per 35 s run, up to 208 ms CONTENDED) sit in the draw phase (pre -> post draw 23-46 ms with render CPU < 1 ms): the swap chain / present blocks, not the game's work. Re-check trigger: any p99 over 8.3 ms uncontended, or a hitch whose process or flush share is over half its wall time.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION perf profile: per-system cost in the live fight comes from `NodeSpans` (dev arena `--spans=<json>`, `project/dev/bench/node_spans.gd`): each processing node is timed between marks in its own process order, and each CanvasItem redraw is timed on the `draw` signal. Godot 4 emits `draw` before the script's `_draw`, so an interval is charged to the item whose signal opened it. The city fight, editor, vsync on, per 60 fps frame (CONTENDED): World loop 1.27 ms, ArenaFloorView _draw 1.00, FxView _draw 0.91, CharacterRigs 0.72, CityView 0.59, ArenaRigs 0.33, everything else under 0.16. Rejected: guessing from the suspect list (KillCharFx looked like 1 ms under the first, signal-order-blind cut; it costs 0.01).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION arena (perf-city, for the owner of `features/arena/`): on the city block, ArenaFloorView draws its static props (barrier runs, cones, crate rows) and crowd rings once onto a `show_behind_parent` child with `use_parent_material`, instead of re-issuing them every frame. The draw order and the print are the same: 420 fixed-fps frames of the reel ch 18 fight are pixel-identical before and after. Floor _draw 1.00 -> 0.69 ms mean, p99 1.70 -> 1.18 (editor, CONTENDED; the other rows rose 8-10 % in the same run). The dock arena keeps the per-frame path (its floor tiles must stay under the props). Re-check trigger: any station prop or spawn that changes after load (the child only redraws on a new stage).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION arena (perf-city, for the owner of `features/arena/`): ArenaFloorView skips a target ring or moving-target track whose box lies wholly outside the on-screen world rect grown by CULL_M = 3 m (`get_global_transform_with_canvas()` against the viewport rect, so a parent transform or a camera is honoured). Nothing it skips could reach a pixel: 420 fixed-fps frames of the reel ch 18 fight are pixel-identical to the pre-change file. Exported release, CONTENDED, interleaved: floor _draw 0.56/0.58 -> 0.24/0.26 ms mean. With the static props print, the floor went 0.77-1.01 -> 0.24-0.26 ms a frame. Re-check trigger: a second viewport or a minimap that shares this canvas's World2D (it would lose the off-screen rings).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION gunplay (perf-city, for the owner of `features/combat/weapons/` and quest 1's FxView): the table gun's ground marks (rifle drops, the fire scorch, plasma scorch rings, the ink scuffs of rounds in the ground; up to ~31 live, 240 ticks each) moved from FxView's per-frame draw into a retained layer, `FxGround` (a `show_behind_parent` child with `use_parent_material`) with one `FxGroundMark` child per mark. A mark is printed once and re-printed only while its fade changes (its last 60 ticks); it calls the same GunFxMarks functions in draw_under's order. About 100 circle polygons a frame were re-issued before, which was 0.4-0.5 ms of FxView's draw. Proof: 900 fixed-fps frames of the reel ch 18 fight (1280x720) with the flag off and on are pixel-identical. Editor, vsync on, CONTENDED, 2 interleaved pairs: FxView draw total 0.91 -> 0.53-0.56 ms mean (p99 of FxView 2.8-2.9 -> 0.8), all spans 4.41 -> 4.04-4.07 ms. The alternative is one value, `FxGround.enabled = false` (the old per-frame path, unchanged). Rejected: off-screen culling (every impact of this fight is on screen: 96847 of 96847 checks). Re-check trigger: a mark whose print depends on more than its fade (e.g. animated scuffs), or two impacts with the same x, y, kind and tick (they would share one mark).
- [2026-11-04] FINDING perf (perf-city): the one 33-35 ms frame that nearly every exported-release city fight shows 1.2-1.9 s after its first frame is bound to the process's age, not to the fight. In 4 release runs it fell at engine time 3934-4161 ms while its offset from the first fight frame varied 1176-1547 ms. It is in the draw/present phase (draw 30 ms, process 2-4 ms, no pipeline compile). In the editor build the first frame comes ~5 s in and the stall is hidden in the load. FightFrames logs `engine_ms_at_first` for this. Not fixed: no game work to remove. In the shipped boot the first ~4 s are title and loading. Re-check if a gameplay frame ever starts before engine time 4.5 s.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION rivet-arsenal (perf-city, for the owner of `features/player/` and weapon-art): RivetHeldView.warm() runs `WeaponArt.make_held` (then frees it) plus `part_texture` (mag, tank) for every gun in Rivet's carry slots in `_ready`, behind the load. WeaponArt loads a texture from disk on first use, so Rivet's first draw of a new gun cost an 8-19 ms process frame in every city fight run (tick 2019 of reel ch 18, 18 of 18 logs). After: 3.8 ms that frame, held view max 18.4 -> 1.9 ms. The textures and sprites are the same, so the pixels are the same. Not warmed: guns picked up off the ground (not in the slots at load). Rejected: warming every weapon-art row (memory and load time for guns never carried).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION fx blood (perf-city, for the owner of `features/fx/` (d5-combat, gunplay)): CorpseStains' soaks print in ONE draw call each. `CircleFan` (features/fx/noir/circle_fan.gd) records the soak's circles (`BloodMarks.splat_into`, the same expressions in the same order as `splat()`) and prints them as one `canvas_item_add_triangle_array` made of the engine's own circle (a 64-segment fan in float32). Godot issues one draw call per `draw_circle` (a polygon command; the canvas batcher does not merge them), and a corpse soak is 11 spots x ~12 circles. Riot bench (`--blood` A/B, draw calls are load-independent): noir 1759 / ink 623 / stains off 658 / **fixed 673** draw calls a frame; render CPU 1.38 -> 0.73 ms (editor, CONTENDED). This was the d-combat riot regression the 2026-11-03 bisect pinned on CityBlood (avg frame 1.13 -> 2.08 ms): its scripts are only ~0.1-0.3 ms a frame, and the draw calls were the rest. Proof: the real CorpseStains with the blood print material, 16 soaks at scales 0.3-1.05, 1.5x zoom at city px ~4800,2900, flag off vs on: 0 of 921600 pixels differ (evidence/perf/riot9). `splat()` keeps its per-circle draws for BloodDecals and BloodStains (same calls through a recorder). The alternative is one value: `CircleFan.batched = false`. Rejected: fewer corpse spots (changes the look), a cached texture per soak (resampling changes pixels). Re-check trigger: an engine upgrade that changes `canvas_item_add_circle` (segment count, antialiasing): re-run the compare.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION tooling (perf-city, for every lane): `tools/lint/lint.mjs` runs gdformat and gdlint in batches of 200 files. With 810 GDScript files the single command line passed Windows' 32767-character limit: the spawn failed and gdformat FAILed naming no file. A planted misformatted file is still caught (FAIL gdformat names it). Rejected: a response file (gdtoolkit takes none). Re-check trigger: none; a batch has the same exit rule as one call.
### 2026-11-04 combat notes (day lane d6-combat-notes)

- [2026-11-04] DECISION homing shard reaction (owner note, reel ch 14 "character reacts with delay"): the cause was the view, not the sim. The killing shard lands on the kill tick, but (a) its 1.2 m splash drew as the rocket's burst (cream starburst, then the smoke ring) over the victim for about 0.6 s, and (b) brutality's burst row holds the cut body frozen for hitstop 3 + grind 36 ticks before the pop, so all the eye saw was a late pop. Now a blast under `FightScorch.MIN_BLAST_M` draws `FightShardArt.pop` (cream splinters and a thin ink ring, 9 ticks, nothing filled over the body), and the held body flinches on the tick each drawn shard lands (`KillShardJolt.HITS` 0/7/15/22/30: head and arms thrown off the beat, the feet planted, alternating lean, a tremble to the pop); the drawn shards appear on those ticks. View only: no sim, replay or parity change. The alternative is one flag: `--no-shard-flinch` (both back, the A/B's before), or `KillShardJolt.SNAP_M` 0 / `FightShardArt.own_pop` false. Rejected: shortening the burst row's grind (the owner approved the brutality look) and moving the kill to the last shard (a sim change for a view problem).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION electro reaction (owner note "improve character reaction on electro gun"): the stun family's convulsion is now stepped, not smooth: `ShockJolt.pose` holds each channel (body x/y, torso, hands up, reach, head turn, head x/y, the hat) for 1/RATES s (7-23 steps a second, primes apart) and then jumps, so the limbs jolt off each other's rhythm and the head and hat snap; the charged kill's death_shock convulses the same way before its stiff fall (its X-ray flash and plank stay). Cream arcs in an ink rim crawl over a stunned body (`ShockArcsView` over the rigs, centred on the rig's torso: the fight's fx layer sits under the rigs, where they were hidden). View only, no sim change. Flag: `--soft-shock` = before. The pistol drops: on the first stun tick `ShockArcsView` tosses an inked pistol out of the gun hand (8 ticks, a hop and a spin) to lie beside him until the stun ends (the rig's hand cell already lets go). The charged kill's corpse keeps the dropped pistol beside the plank (the drop is kept for a dead victim whose rig the cut hid); it goes when the fighter leaves the fight (corpse decay), not as the body inks out (a known gap).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION plasma kill reaction (owner note "improve character reaction on ... plasma rifle"): the holed body is thrown back along the bolt sooner and further (`KillHoleFx.LOOK_TICKS` 8 -> 3, `FALL_M` 0.45 -> 0.85 m) and the wound smokes (5 pale ash puffs in an ink rim rising out of the hole, 300 ticks; `SMOKE_TICKS` 0 = none). The hole and its glow stay as approved. View only. Flag `--no-plasma-heat` = the night's values and no smoke (the A/B's before; its frame 120 is byte-identical to a render of main's code). Not done: a kick on the hit tick itself (the stagger below covers the non-lethal hit).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION plasma wound (owner note "on non-lethal hits (armored), a stagger before the drop"): a plasma bolt that does not kill plays `stagger` (0.55 s, the step back along the hit, the gun arm dropping) instead of the medium class's 0.24 s `flinch`, from the hit frame: the new optional row `human_reactions.json` `weapon_wound` (`plasma_rifle: stagger`, read by `ReactTable.weapon_wound`, played by `ReactLink._heavy`). The body is marked: `PlasmaScorchView` draws a charred spot on the torso's entry side with a hot cream rim cooling to an ember over 120 ticks and a thin smoke column for 150 ticks, riding the stagger (last 3 marks kept, gone at death where the kill's hole takes over). A riot shield still takes the bolt (sparks, `blocked`), the bearer staggers behind it, unmarked. View only, no sim change. Flag: `--no-plasma-heat` also turns this off (one before for the whole plasma note); the alternative is removing the `weapon_wound` row. Filmed on a new director chapter `plasma_armored` (armored hp, no shield look, tap fire 1 bolt per 50 ticks: at held 6 Hz fire the second bolt lands 10 ticks after the first and the stagger only shows for those 10 ticks, which is the game's true behaviour under held fire). Rejected: raising plasma's hit_class to heavy (moves the sim's knockback and hit-stop: a replay/parity change for a view note).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION Rivet's melee swing (owner note "player's hold and animation of melee weapons"): the cause of the flat swing was timing. The sim lands a melee hit on the first active tick, the night's view (`RivetHeldView._sweep`) began the blade's cut on that same tick, and the hit-stop then froze the blade still cocked, so every slash read as late. Now `MeleeSwing.angle` cocks the blade past the hit's arc (`ANTIC` 1.3, eased out), starts the cut `CUT_TICKS` 2 before the windup ends so the blade crosses the aim line on the hit tick (the hit-stop now holds it in the target), overshoots to `FOLLOW` 1.35 on the far side, holds there for `HOLD` 0.35 of the recovery, then eases home. `MeleeSmearView` draws the cut as a pale fan between the blade's last 4 angles (hilt to tip, with an ink rim; hot cream for the plasma sword, spark white for the baton) that wipes off in about 3 frames. The held chainsaw judders while idle (`MeleeSwing.idle_judder`, tick-locked, 0.04 rad), not only while biting. View only: the sim's windup/active/recovery ticks are unchanged (no replay or parity change). Flag `--soft-swing` = the night's sweep, no smear, no idle judder. Rejected: lengthening the sim's windup ticks for more anticipation (a gameplay and replay change). Not done yet: the two-handed grip (the off claw on the hilt) and the guard at the claw.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION two-handed melee grip (owner note "two-handed for the chainsaw and the two-hand blades"): stays OFF by default behind `--two-hand-grip`. The geometry is done: for the katana and the plasma sword the gun claw holds the hilt in front of the chest (`RigGrip.HILT_FWD`, `HILT_ACROSS`; at Rivet's side the hilt was out of the off arm's reach: shoulders 316 part px apart, reach 246) and the off wrist closes on the hilt along the blade (`CutRig.hilt_dir`, 0-4 px in `test_two_hand_hilt`). But Rivet's off arm has no claw cell: its forearm texture is a 434 px torch tool past a 182 px wrist, so with the wrist on the hilt the tool draws on across the blade (`dev/combat_notes/hilt_probe.gd`). That is rig art, which waits for today's proportion redesign. Re-check trigger: the redesigned Rivet lands with an off hand (or the owner accepts one-claw blades and the flag is deleted). The chainsaw already holds at the hip in both arms (`heavy_two_hand` = HIP). Rejected: squashing the torch forearm so its tip lands on the hilt (a stubby, distorted tool) and laying it along the blade (a second blade).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION plasma kill kick (owner note "the victim thrown back along the bolt"; closes the "not done" of the plasma kill decision above): on the hit tick the holed body snaps `KillHoleFx.KICK_M` 0.3 m back along the shot, stretched `KICK_STRETCH` 0.14, and holds it through the look; the topple carries it on to `FALL_M`. At 0.18 m the impact flash hid the move at 64 px/m (filmed, rejected). View only, no sim change. Flag `--no-plasma-heat` = no kick (the night's throw).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION claw jab timing (owner note "animation of melee weapons", same cause as the blade swing): `RigClips._strike_reach` left the striking arm pulled back (-0.35) on the first active tick, the tick the sim lands the hit, and the hit-stop froze it cocked. Now the jab starts `RigClips.JAB_TICKS` 2 before the windup ends and is at full reach on the hit tick, then holds through the active frames. It applies to every rig strike (Rivet's claw and the arm under his blades). View only, no sim change. Flag `--soft-swing` = the night's curve (`RigClips.jab_early`). Filmed 16:12 (`claw_jab_ab_64ppm_slow.mp4`, 0.2x at 2x zoom): the claw shows past the victim's chest on the hit frames, where the night's showed nothing; modest, because Rivet stands inside the victim at contact in the claw_combo chapter.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION melee smear linger (amends the swing decision's "wipes off in about 3 frames"): a finished cut's fan stays whole `MeleeSmearView.LINGER` 4 frames while it fades, then wipes one angle a frame. At 48 px/m real time the 2-tick baton cut flickered (about 5 frames on screen); now about 9. View only. Alternative: `LINGER` 0 = the iteration-3 wipe. Rejected: more kept angles (`KEEP`) - a 2-tick cut only has 3 angles to keep.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION plasma scorch size (amends the plasma wound decision): `PlasmaScorchView.SPOT_M` 0.1 -> 0.13 m, so the mark is 6 px at 48 px/m and reads at real time, not only through its smoke. Rejected: an entry flare ring on the hit tick (doubled the impact ring that already marks the hit frame). Alternative: `SPOT_M` 0.1.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION electro spits (owner note "must read as electrocuted at 32 px/m"): besides the crawling arcs on the body, the stun draws `ShockJolt.SPITS` 3 short kinked arcs from 0.16 m out to 0.5 m off the torso centre, re-aimed every other tick. At 32 px/m the human is about 20 px and the crawling arcs stayed inside his silhouette (filmed: `all_notes_ab_32ppm.mp4`, the tap read barely); the spits reach about 16 px out and make the stun read as live current. View only. Flag `--soft-shock` = none (with the rest of the shock). Alternative: `SPITS` 0. Rejected: longer crawling arcs (they cross the face and hide the jolted head).
- [2026-11-04] DECISION claw over the victim (owner note "animation of melee weapons"; the claw jab was the weakest point of the 32 px/m reel): while a timed strike extends (`c_reach > 0`), the striking arm's z is lifted `RigArm.STRIKE_Z` +20, so it draws over every part of another rig (a human's head is z 12) and under the held weapon (+60). At contact Rivet stands inside the cop, and the night drew the claw under the cop's head and arms. `--soft-swing` = the night's z. Rejected: lifting Rivet's whole rig (his torso would cover the victim's reaction and blood) and a forward lunge (more overlap, not less). Re-check when the redesigned slimmer rigs land: the overlap may shrink.
- [2026-11-04] DECISION melee kill death (owner note "animation of melee weapons"; the claw overlap was the weakest point left): Rivet's melee kills (PlayerMelee through `CombatArena.damage`) emit `kill_landed` but no `struck`, and ReactLink played deaths only from `struck`. So no claw or electro-baton kill ever played a death: the victim dropped into the rig's default X-sprawl where he stood, often under Rivet. Katana, chainsaw and plasma sword were covered by their signature kills. Now ReactLink queues the melee death from `kill_landed` for `MELEE_IDS`, along Rivet -> victim, and `death_melee` `travel_m` is 0.9 (was 0.5, never seen), so the body folds over the blow and topples clear. View only, no replay change. Rejected: emitting `struck` from PlayerMelee (it drives HitLink's sim knockback and hit-stop: a replay change and a re-bless). Flag `--soft-swing` (`ReactLink.melee_deaths` off) = the night's. Alternative: `travel_m` 0.5.

- [2026-11-04] DECISION look riso (owner on targets r2_a5: "Chase middleground between this variant and current game"; "Tone down graininess of the surface but do not remove completely"): the riso layer lives inside the existing D5 overlay shader (`look_d5.gdshader` riso_print: a world-locked 45-degree halftone at 4 screen px in the mid-tone fills with the mean tone kept, the colour plate printed 1 px off register as a ghost plus a paper sliver, short fragmented paper-fibre strands), not as a second full-screen pass (no extra BackBufferCopy). The whole layer scales with one dial, `look_d5.params.json` `world_riso.riso` (0 = the old print bit-identical); grain 0.5 -> 0.3. `look_profiles.json` overlay `d5_riso` drives `--look=auto` and `d5`; the old print stays as `--look=today` / `d5_today` for A/B. Red stays out of it (the halftone and the plate skip vermilion and blood). Rejected: unbroken ridge-noise fibres (read as a contour map), anisotropic fibres (read as rain, d5-close). Re-check: the owner's verdict on the dial value; the target is still warmer (sepia paper, stains) than the middle ground.

The default boss walk sidesteps 45 ticks when cover cancels its step (the gang was pinned behind crate rows). Boss bodies meet Rivet by a bodies.json class (`Fighter.body_class`, bosses.json `body_class`: King `boss`, the gang `robot`; both block) instead of as people; Kind.BOSS alone was not used (quest 1's Mertz is Kind.BOSS and a human). - [2026-11-06] DECISION view lab (n7x-view it2): an alternative camera is one launch flag `--view=<id>` over `data/tables/view.json` (gtai.view/1; `core/render/view_mode.gd`); the default `top` is the shipped 90-degree orthographic view and applies no transform (`is_flat()`), so goldens, replay and parity do not move (the seed-2 tick-900 arena frame is md5-identical before and after). A tilt is one canvas transform (Camera2D zoom `(z, z sin pitch)`: ground, decals, FX, lights, markers and the mouse's ground point all follow it) plus per-node height lifts (`ViewMode.lift_px`). Rejected: a full-screen post-warp (bends the print grain and the HUD), a Node3D camera (no 3D path).

- [2026-11-06] DECISION bosses/king difficulty (n7-king it20): King stays in the easy band as built (call C). Measured with the human-like bot, seeds 1-10, n=10 per variant (`evidence/king/numbers-it20-*`): hits raised to the 30-hp floor, a 48-degree coin fan, cooldowns cut 20-35 % and the gang at 11 all give 10/10 survival, and so does a bot reading only 50 % of tells, because Rivet's repair (`combat.json rivet.repair_per_s` 10 after 3 s) heals between spaced hits. Fair needs a law change (a hit over 30, or a boss-only repair delay), which is the owner's call, not a number. Rejected: x1.4 damage (the ram at 42 breaks the moveset's floor; run stopped unmeasured).
- [2026-11-06] DECISION bosses/servo n7-servo difficulty (it22): Servo's row does not set n7-king's `rivet_repair_delay_s` (the fight keeps combat.json's 3 s, the easy band). Measured with the human bot at n = 10 on the merged tree (temporary edits, reverted): 6 s = the it20 fights exactly, 0 deaths; 30 s = a death in 6 of 10 fights, Servo still downed 10/10 (`evidence/servo/numbers-it22-repair6/`, `-repair30/`). Rejected for tonight: picking a value between them (unmeasured, and the band is the owner's: REPORT call 21). Re-check: the owner's verdict on King's call C and Servo's call 21.
- [2026-11-06] DECISION bosses rivet_repair_delay_s (n7-king it22): an optional generic boss field (`bosses.json <id>.rivet_repair_delay_s`, 0-30 s; absent = combat.json's 3 s) sets Rivet's repair delay for that fight on the stage's own CombatTuning; dev flag `--repair-delay=<s>` overrides it. It is call C's lever: it makes spaced boss hits add up without lifting the 30-hp floor. Measured (human-like bot, seeds 1-10, n=10 each, `evidence/king/numbers-it22-*`): 8 s 10/10 without a death, 12 s 9/10, 16 s 8/10, 20 s 7/10. King ships without it (the easy band stays the default, the owner's call); nothing moves. Rejected: lifting the 30-hp floor (a law; one heavy hit punishes a single misread instead of a run of them) and a global combat.json change (it would move every arena fight and the director reel).

- [2026-11-06] DECISION godot-runner: budget gates know about contention. Every runGodot registers in a machine-wide ledger (%LOCALAPPDATA%/gtai/runs); run_scenarios tells Godot how many other runs are active (GTAI_CONTENTION + a live file) and a miss of the two wall-clock budgets (city_chase nav 2 ms, rig_stress p50 1 ms) under contention logs CONTENDED and passes, then those scenarios are re-run alone once the machine is quiet (up to 5 min) and judged strictly; an idle-machine miss FAILS and a machine that never quiets reports CONTENDED (not PASS, not FAIL). The bench queues FIFO behind other runs and bench.lock with an ETA, running flagged CONTENDED after 20 min. Rejected: blocking all other lanes while the bench runs (no lane-side hook; would stall 15 lanes for 14 min). Re-check: if CONTENDED verdicts stay unresolved for days, add a lane-side "bench running" gate. Also: runGodot auto-imports a stale class cache once per process (tools/lib/class_cache.mjs), tools/godot/check.mjs is the safe --check-only preflight (--check-only cannot see autoloads: those false positives are filtered).
- [2026-11-06] DECISION evidence: `release_compile` (does what a release export ships still compile, dev/ tests/ addons/gut dropped) is written by `tools/evidence/run.mjs` as the existing `export-audit` kind, skippable with `--no-release`; the `pof.godot-evidence/1` envelope and its kind enum are unchanged (same rule as the F-12 scenario evidence). The PCK audit (EXP-05) joins this kind when it lands. Rejected: a new `release-compile` kind (changes the PoF wire contract).
- [2026-11-06] DECISION test-suite: `tools/mutate/mutate.mjs` is the way to answer "do my tests bite on this file?" (TST-05): it plants single-line mutants (comparison, boundary, arithmetic, literal, boolean, statement deletion, bool return) in ONE GDScript file inside a dedicated git worktree outside the repo (`<tmpdir>/gtai-mutate-<hash of the main root>`, marker-stamped, reused warm), runs the test_map-mapped fast-tier tests per mutant and reports survivors first plus killed/(killed+survived); invalid (does not compile) and timeout are not scored. The tool never deletes with anything but `git worktree remove --force <exact path>` behind marker + under-tmpdir + not-root/not-lane checks, and restores files from in-memory bytes + a `.gtai-orig` sidecar a crashed run repairs. First real run: core/save/gtsv_container.gd 43 mutants, 34 killed, 9 survived (79.1%), 292 s; it exposed a real gap (encode never tested for full float precision). Lanes quote the score instead of "N planted faults caught" (hand-written negative controls stay valid but are not a score). Rejected: mutating the main checkout in place (the project/ wipe incident), running the full suite per mutant, mutating working-tree changes (HEAD only, commit first). Re-check when a native C++ mutation story is wanted or the per-mutant cost (about 6.5 s, one Godot start each) matters.

Wired by a station-only ArenaHook (`arena_stations/view-pitch.json`, `ViewPitchHook`): each live card gets an overlay child drawing the p70 strip at the base card's height and anchor fraction (RigHeadCard sizes by cell width, which drew the wider 70 King bust at 68 %), until lane chars resolves `art` in RigHeadCard itself (REQUEST). Rejected: hard-coding 55 or 70 paths in RigHeadCard; a second CARDS table per pitch.

The ease steps on sim ticks, so a hit-stop holds it. The cards cross-fade from the p70 to the base art over `fade_deg` 5 centred on `swap_deg` 62.5 (`fade_deg` 0 = a hard swap). 55-only = `tilt`, 70-only = `tilt70`: one value away.
